「好啊，我就乐意做那种年轻人！」我叫道。「另一路人我见得太多了——就在那班对大人您阿谀奉承、甚至肯屈尊奉承我的年轻辩护士身上。年长的那些人身上我也见过。他们一个个全为着旁门私利，整个一族都是！正因这个，我才显得信不过大人的情分。我凭什么想您会喜欢我？可您亲口说过，您有自己的利害！」
说到这里我收了口，惊觉自己竟跑得这样远；他正拿一张深不可测的脸看着我。
「大人，恕罪，」我又开口。「我这两片腮帮子里没有别的，只有一条乡下粗舌头。我想，去探望囹圄中的朋友，不过是人之常情；可我欠着您一条命，这一点我永不忘记；若是为了大人您的好处，我就留在这儿。这不过是知恩罢了。」
「这几句话，本可用更少的字说到。」普雷斯顿格兰奇冷冷地说。

===Original===

“It is easy, and it is at times gracious, to say a plain Scots ‘ay.’ ”
“Ah, but, my lord, I think ye take me not yet entirely!” cried I. “For *your* sake, for my life-safe, and the kindness that ye say ye bear to me﻿—for these, I’ll consent; but not for any good that might be coming to myself. If I stand aside when this young maid is in her trial, it’s a thing I will be noways advantaged by; I will lose by it, I will never gain. I would rather make a shipwreck wholly than to build on that foundation.”
He was a minute serious, then smiled. “You mind me of the man with the long nose,” said he: “was you to look at the moon by a telescope, you would see David Balfour there! But you shall have your way of it. I will ask at you one service, and then set you free. My clerks are overdriven; be so good as copy me these few pages,” says he, visibly swithering among some huge rolls of manuscripts, “and when that is done, I shall bid you God speed! I would never charge myself with Mr. David’s conscience; and if you could cast some part of it (as you went by) in a moss hag, you would find yourself to ride much easier without it.”
“Perhaps not just entirely in the same direction though, my lord!” says I.
“And you shall have the last word, too!” cries he gaily.
Indeed he had some cause for gaiety, having now found the means to gain his purpose. To lessen the weight of the memorial, or to have a readier answer at his hand, he desired I should appear publicly in the character of his intimate. But if I were to appear with the same publicity as a visitor to Catriona in her prison the world would scarce stint to draw conclusions, and the true nature of James More’s escape must become evident to all. This was the little problem I had set him of a sudden, and to which he had so briskly found an answer. I was to be tethered in Glasgow by that job of copying, which in mere outward decency I could not well refuse; and during these hours of my employment Catriona was privately got rid of. I think shame to write of this man that loaded me with so many goodnesses. He was kind to me as any father, yet I ever thought him as false as a cracked bell.

===Chinese===

「一句苏格兰土腔的『是』，说出口容易，有时还体面。」
「啊，可是大人，我看您还没全明白我！」我叫道。「为*您*，为我这条保下的命，为您说自己对我的那番好意——为这些，我甘愿留下；可不为任何将落到我头上的好处。这位年轻姑娘受难的当口我若袖手旁观，于我有百害而无一利；我只会失，永不会得。我宁可整个儿船沉，也不在那块地基上盖楼。」
他正色了一分钟，随即笑了。「你叫我想起那个长鼻子的人，」他说，「拿望远镜看月亮，准能在上头瞧见戴维·鲍尔弗！也罢，就依你的路数。我只求你效一回劳，然后便放你自由。我的书办们忙不过来；劳驾替我抄这几页，」他说，在一大堆手稿卷子中间明明白白踌躇了一番，「抄完了，我便祝你一路平安！戴维先生的良心，我可决计不敢领受；你若能顺路把它（哪怕一部分）丢进一个泥炭坑里，你会发现自己骑起马来轻快得多。」
「只怕丢的方向不见得同您指的完全一致，大人！」我说。
「末一个字也让你占去！」他快活地叫道。
他的快活确有来由：他已找到了达到目的的手段。为减轻陈情书的分量，或为手边备一个现成的回话，他要我以亲信的身份公开露面。可我若再同样公开地去狱中探望卡特琳娜，世人难免不生推想，詹姆斯·莫尔「越狱」的真相便要大白于天下。这是我突然出给他的一道小难题，而他应手便解了：用那桩抄写的差使把我拴在格拉斯哥——单看情面，这差使我无法推辞；而在我伏案效劳的那几个钟头里，卡特琳娜被悄悄地弄走了。想起此人待我的万千好处，写下这些我实在惭愧。他待我亲如父亲，可我始终觉得他虚伪得像一口裂了纹的钟。

## XXVI The Threesome / 二十六 三人相处
===Original===

Whether or not I was to be so much blamed, or rather perhaps pitied, I must leave others to judge of. My shrewdness (of which I have a good deal, too) seems not so great with the ladies. No doubt, at the moment when I awaked her, I was thinking a good deal of the effect upon James More; and similarly when I returned and we were all sat down to breakfast, I continued to behave to the young lady with deference and distance; as I still think to have been most wise. Her father had cast doubts upon the innocence of my friendship; and these, it was my first business to allay. But there is a kind of an excuse for Catriona also. We had shared in a scene of some tenderness and passion, and given and received caresses; I had thrust her from me with violence; I had called aloud upon her in the night from the one room to the other; she had passed hours of wakefulness and weeping; and it is not to be supposed I had been absent from her pillow thoughts. Upon the back of this, to be awaked, with unaccustomed formality, under the name of Miss Drummond, and to be thenceforth used with a great deal of distance and respect, led her entirely in error on my private sentiments; and she was indeed so incredibly abused as to imagine me repentant and trying to draw off!
The trouble betwixt us seems to have been this: that whereas I (since I had first set eyes on his great hat) thought singly of James More, his return and suspicions, she made so little of these that I may say she scarce remarked them, and all her troubles and doings regarded what had passed between us in the night before. This is partly to be explained by the innocence and boldness of her character; and partly because James More, having sped so ill in his interview with me, or had his mouth closed by my invitation, said no word to her upon the subject. At the breakfast, accordingly, it soon appeared we were at cross purposes. I had looked to find her in clothes of her own: I found her (as if her father were forgotten) wearing some of the best that I had bought for her and which she knew (or thought) that I admired her in.

===Chinese===

我该受这许多责备呢，还是更该受怜悯，只好留给旁人评断。我的精明（这份精明我原也不少）到了姑娘们身上，似乎就不大灵光。毫无疑问，唤醒她那一刻，我心里正转着许多对詹姆斯·莫尔之效果的盘算；同样，等我回来、大家落座吃早饭时，我对这位年轻小姐也始终执礼恭谨、保持距离；如今我仍认为这是最明智的做法。她父亲既已对我的友谊之清白投了疑影，我的头一件要务便是把这疑影抹平。可卡特琳娜那边，也有她的情有可原。我们曾同处过一幕带着温存与热切的情景，彼此给过、也受过爱抚；我曾粗暴地把她推开；曾在夜里从一个房间朝另一个房间高声唤她；她曾熬过几个小时的失眠和哭泣；想来我不曾离开过她枕边的思绪。紧接这一切之后，忽然被人一本正经地唤醒，唤作德拉蒙德小姐，从此又被以极多的距离和敬意相待，这便叫她把我的心意完全猜岔了；她当真是被坑害到了难以置信的地步，竟以为我在悔恨，想要抽身！
我们之间的龃龉，症结似乎在这儿：我呢，自打头一眼望见他那顶大帽子，心里便只有詹姆斯·莫尔——他的归来、他的疑心；她呢，却把这些看得轻之又轻，简直可以说视而不见，她全部的苦恼和举止，都系在我们昨夜之间发生的事情上。这一半可以拿她天性里的天真与胆大来解释；另一半则因为詹姆斯·莫尔同我那场会谈败得那样惨——或者说，嘴已被我的邀请封住了——对这一节对她只字不提。于是到了早饭桌上，我们各说各话的情形立刻显了形。我原以为她会穿自己的衣裳：却见她（仿佛把父亲忘了个干净）穿着我给她买的那几身最好的——她知道（或自以为知道）我爱看她穿的那些。

I had looked to find her imitate my affectation of distance, and be most precise and formal; instead I found her flushed and wild-like, with eyes extraordinary bright, and a painful and varying expression, calling me by name with a sort of appeal of tenderness, and referring and deferring to my thoughts and wishes like an anxious or a suspected wife.
But this was not for long. As I beheld her so regardless of her own interests, which I had jeopardised and was now endeavoring to recover, I redoubled my own boldness in the manner of a lesson to the girl. The more she came forward, the further I drew back; the more she betrayed the closeness of our intimacy, the more pointedly civil I became, until even her father (if he had not been so engrossed with eating) might have observed the opposition. In the midst of which, of a sudden, she became wholly changed, and I told myself, with a good deal of relief, that she had took the hint at last.
All day I was at my classes or in quest of my new lodging; and though the hour of our customary walk hung miserably on my hands, I cannot say but I was happy on the whole to find my way cleared, the girl again in proper keeping, the father satisfied or at least acquiescent, and myself free to prosecute my love with honour. At supper, as at all our meals, it was James More that did the talking. No doubt but he talked well, if anyone could have believed him. But I will speak of him presently more at large. The meal at an end, he rose, got his great coat, and looking (as I thought) at me, observed he had affairs abroad. I took this for a hint that I was to be going also, and got up; whereupon the girl, who had scarce given me greeting at my entrance, turned her eyes on me wide open, with a look that bade me stay. I stood between them like a fish out of water, turning from one to the other; neither seemed to observe me, she gazing on the floor, he buttoning his coat: which vastly swelled my embarrassment. This appearance of indifferency argued, upon her side, a good deal of anger very near to burst out. Upon his, I thought it horribly alarming; I made sure there was a tempest brewing there; and considering that to be the chief peril, turned towards him and put myself (so to speak) in the man’s hands.
“Can I do anything for *you*, Mr. Drummond?” says I.
He stifled a yawn, which again I thought to be duplicity. “Why, Mr.

可这没有持续多久。眼见她这般不顾自己的名节——那名节已被我拖累，如今正由我竭力挽回——我便把姿态愈发加码，算是对姑娘的一点教训。她越往前凑，我越往后撤；她越显出我们之间的亲近，我越拿出彬彬有礼的架子，直到连她父亲（若不是他埋头大嚼）都该看出这番对峙了。正对峙着，她陡然间整个人变了样；我大为宽慰地对自己说，她总算领会了暗示。
一整天我不是在上课便是寻我的新住处；我们惯常散步的钟点虽然悬在手上、熬得难受，总的说来我不能不感到宽慰：路扫清了，姑娘重新有了妥当的监护，父亲满意了——至少默许了，而我也可以体体面面地继续我的爱情。晚饭桌上，一如每顿饭，说话的都是詹姆斯·莫尔。不用说，他说得漂亮——只要有人肯信。关于他，我稍后再细说。饭一吃完，他便起身，取过大衣，望着（我以为是我）说外头有事。我把这当作暗示我该走了，也站起身；那姑娘——我进门时她几乎没同我招呼——闻声把眼睛睁得大大地转向我，那眼神叫我留下。我夹在他们两人中间，活像条离水的鱼，从这张脸转向那张脸；两人似乎都没瞧见我：她盯着地板，他扣着衣扣——这更把我的窘迫鼓得满满的。她那副冷淡的样子，说明一腔怒气已憋到爆发的边上；他那副样子，我想想更觉吓人：我断定那里正酝酿着一场风暴；既然认定这才是头号危险，我便转向他，把自己（可以说）交到这人手里。
「我能为*您*效点什么劳么，德拉蒙德先生？」我说。
他压下一个呵欠——我又疑心这是作假。「哦，

===Original===

David,” said he, “since you are so obliging as to propose it, you might show me the way to a certain tavern” (of which he gave the name) “where I hope to fall in with some old companions in arms.”
There was no more to say, and I got my hat and cloak to bear him company.
“And as for you,” he says to his daughter, “you had best go to your bed. I shall be late home, and ‘Early to bed and early to rise, gars bonny lasses have bright eyes.’ ”
Whereupon he kissed her with a good deal of tenderness, and ushered me before him from the door. This was so done (I thought on purpose) that it was scarce possible there should be any parting salutation; but I observed she did not look at me, and set it down to terror of James More.
It was some distance to that tavern. He talked all the way of matters which did not interest me the smallest, and at the door dismissed me with empty manners. Thence I walked to my new lodging, where I had not so much as a chimney to hold me warm, and no society but my own thoughts. These were still bright enough; I did not so much as dream that Catriona was turned against me; I thought we were like folk pledged; I thought we had been too near and spoke too warmly to be severed, least of all by what were only steps in a most needful policy. And the chief of my concern was only the kind of father-in-law that I was getting, which was not at all the kind I would have chosen: and the matter of how soon I ought to speak to him, which was a delicate point on several sides. In the first place, when I thought how young I was, I blushed all over, and could almost have found it in my heart to have desisted; only that if once I let them go from Leyden without explanation, I might lose her altogether. And in the second place, there was our very irregular situation to be kept in view, and the rather scant measure of satisfaction I had given James More that morning. I concluded, on the whole, that delay would not hurt anything, yet I would not delay too long neither; and got to my cold bed with a full heart.
The next day, as James More seemed a little on the complaining hand in the matter of my chamber, I offered to have in more furniture; and coming in the afternoon, with porters bringing chairs and tables, found the girl once more left to herself. She greeted me on my admission civilly, but withdrew at once to her own room, of which she shut the door.

===Chinese===

大卫，」他说，「你既然热心提了出来，就烦你给我引引路，到一家酒馆去」（他说了那家的名字）「我希望在那儿撞见几位旧日的袍泽。」
再无可说，我便取了帽子和斗篷陪他去。
「至于你，」他对女儿说，「最好上床去睡。我要很晚才回来——『早睡早起，姑娘的眼睛才水灵灵的。』」
说罢他颇为温存地吻了她，随即让我先行，跟出门去。这一手安排得（我疑心是故意的）叫人几乎无从作别；我还留意到她没看我，便把这归因于她对詹姆斯·莫尔的惧怕。
到那家酒馆有相当一段路。他一路谈论些我毫无兴趣的事，到了门口，拿几句空客套把我打发了。从那里我走回新居——屋里连个取暖的壁炉都没有，作伴的只有我自己的心思。这心思倒还明亮；我做梦也没想到卡特琳娜会转而与我生分；我以为我们已如定情之人；我以为我们曾经那样亲近、说过那样炽热的话，是拆不散的，更不至于被这些纯属必要权宜的步骤拆散。我主要操心的只有两件：一件是我将要到手的这位岳丈是哪路人——同我情愿挑选的那路毫不相干；另一件是我该多快向他开口——这在好几个方面都是个微妙关口。第一，想到自己多么年轻，我浑身涨红，几乎要打退堂鼓；只是转念一想，一旦放他们不作交代便离开莱顿，我兴许就永远失去她了。第二，我们这番极不合规矩的处境也得放在眼里，何况那天早晨我给詹姆斯·莫尔的满意又那么稀薄。总的掂量下来，拖延不致误事，却也不可拖得太久；于是我揣着一颗满满的心，上了那张冷床。
第二天，詹姆斯·莫尔对我那间屋子似乎微有抱怨之意，我便提出添置些家具；下午我领着脚夫抬桌搬椅进门，发现那姑娘又一次独自被留下。我一进门她客气地招呼了，随即退回自己房里，关上了门。

===Original===

I made my disposition, and paid and dismissed the men so that she might hear them go, when I supposed she would at once come forth again to speak to me. I waited yet awhile, then knocked upon her door.
“Catriona!” said I.
The door was opened so quickly, even before I had the word out, that I thought she must have stood behind it listening. She remained there in the interval quite still; but she had a look that I cannot put a name on, as of one in a bitter trouble.
“Are we not to have our walk today either?” so I faltered.
“I am thanking you,” said she. “I will not be caring much to walk, now that my father is come home.”
“But I think he has gone out himself and left you here alone,” said I.
“And do you think that was very kindly said?” she asked.
“It was not unkindly meant,” I replied. “What ails you, Catriona? What have I done to you that you should turn from me like this?”
“I do not turn from you at all,” she said, speaking very carefully. “I will ever be grateful to my friend that was good to me; I will ever be his friend in all that I am able. But now that my father James More is come again, there is a difference to be made, and I think there are some things said and done that would be better to be forgotten. But I will ever be your friend in all that I am able, and if that is not all that﻿ ﻿… if it is not so much.﻿ ﻿… Not that you will be caring! But I would not have you think of me too hard. It was true what you said to me, that I was too young to be advised, and I am hoping you will remember I was just a child. I would not like to lose your friendship, at all events.”
She began this very pale; but before she was done, the blood was in her face like scarlet, so that not her words only, but her face and the trembling of her very hands, besought me to be gentle. I saw for the first time, how very wrong I had done to place the child in that position, where she had been entrapped into a moment’s weakness, and now stood before me like a person shamed.
“Miss Drummond,” I said, and stuck, and made the same beginning once again, “I wish you could see into my heart,” I cried. “You would read there that my respect is undiminished. If that were possible, I should say it was increased. This is but the result of the mistake we made; and had to come; and the less said of it now the better.

我指挥着安顿妥当，付了钱，把脚夫们打发走，好叫她听见人走了；我以为她随即就会出来同我说话。又等了一阵，我才去敲她的门。
「卡特琳娜！」我说。
门开得那样快——我话音未落便开了——以致我想她必是一直贴在门后听着。她站在门里一动不动；可脸上有一种我说不出名字的神情，像是一个人陷在极大的苦楚里。
「今天……咱们也不去散步了么？」我讷讷地说。
「谢谢你，」她说。「如今父亲回来了，我不大想散步了。」
「可我记得他自己出门去了，把你一个人撂在这里。」我说。
「你觉得这话说得客气么？」她问。
「并无恶意。」我答道。「你怎么了，卡特琳娜？我做了什么，叫你这样嫌弃我？」
「我并没有嫌弃你，」她说得字斟句酌。「对我好的那位朋友，我永远感激；只要我做得到，我永远是他的朋友。可如今我父亲詹姆斯·莫尔回来了，分寸总要有所分别；我想，有些说过的话、做过的事，最好还是忘掉。但我仍愿尽我所能做你的朋友；倘若这不算全部……倘若没有那么多……也并非你在意的！只是我不愿你把我想得太坏。你对我说过的话不错——我年纪太轻，听不进劝；我只盼你记着，我不过是个孩子。无论如何，我不愿失去你的友谊。」
她开口时脸色惨白；话没说完，血已涌上脸，红得像猩红一般；于是不光是她的话，连她的脸、连她那双手的颤抖，都在恳求我温柔相待。我头一次看清，我把这孩子置于那样的境地是何等亏欠——她是在那里被诱入了一瞬的软弱——如今站在我面前，像个蒙羞的人。
「德拉蒙德小姐，」我开了口，卡住了，又从头开始，「但愿你能看见我的心，」我叫道。「你会读到，我的敬意分毫未减。若说可能，我还要说它是增了。这不过是我们所犯错误的结果；早晚要来；如今说得越少越好。

===Original===

Of all of our life here, I promise you it shall never pass my lips; I would like to promise you too that I would never think of it, but it’s a memory that will be always dear to me. And as for a friend, you have one here that would die for you.”
“I am thanking you,” said she.
We stood awhile silent, and my sorrow for myself began to get the upper hand; for here were all my dreams come to a sad tumble, and my love lost, and myself alone again in the world as at the beginning.
“Well,” said I, “we shall be friends always, that’s a certain thing. But this is a kind of a farewell too: it’s a kind of a farewell after all; I shall always ken Miss Drummond, but this is a farewell to my Catriona.”
I looked at her; I could hardly say I saw her, but she seemed to grow great and brighten in my eyes; and with that I suppose I must have lost my head, for I called out her name again and made a step at her with my hands reached forth.
She shrank back like a person struck, her face flamed; but the blood sprang no faster up into her cheeks, than what it flowed back upon my own heart, at sight of it, with penitence and concern. I found no words to excuse myself, but bowed before her very deep, and went my ways out of the house with death in my bosom.
I think it was about five days that followed without any change. I saw her scarce ever but at meals, and then of course in the company of James More. If we were alone even for a moment, I made it my devoir to behave the more distantly and to multiply respectful attentions, having always in my mind’s eye that picture of the girl shrinking and flaming in a blush, and in my heart more pity for her than I could depict in words. I was sorry enough for myself, I need not dwell on that, having fallen all my length and more than all my height in a few seconds; but, indeed, I was near as sorry for the girl, and sorry enough to be scarce angry with her save by fits and starts. Her plea was good: she was but a child; she had been placed in an unfair position; if she had deceived herself and me, it was no more than was to have been looked for.
And for another thing she was now very much alone.

===Chinese===

我们在这里的全部生活，我向你担保，绝不会从我嘴里泄露；我还想向你担保永不回想它——可这记忆，于我将永远亲爱。至于朋友，你眼前就有一个肯为你死的人。」
「谢谢你。」她说。
我们默默站了片刻，我为自己的悲哀渐渐占了上风；眼见我全部的梦碎成一地，我的爱情丢了，我自己又孤零零回到世上，一如起初。
「好吧，」我说，「我们永远是朋友，这是一定的。可这也算是一种道别：毕竟是一种道别；我永远认识德拉蒙德小姐，可这一别，别的是我的卡特琳娜。」
我望着她；与其说看见她，不如说她在我眼里渐渐高大、渐渐发亮；我想我必是失了魂，竟又唤出她的名字，朝她跨出一步，双手探了出去。
她像挨了打似的缩回去，脸烧起来；可血涌上她脸颊有多快，血也以怎样的悔恨与痛惜，随那一眼倒流回我自己心里。我寻不出话为自己开脱，只朝她深深一躬，揣着一片死心出了那座房子。
此后约莫五天，没有一点变化。我几乎只在饭桌上见她，而且自然总有詹姆斯·莫尔作陪。即便单独相对片刻，我也必摆出加倍的疏远、加倍的敬意，因为眼前总晃着那姑娘退缩、烧红着脸的景象，心里对她的怜惜，多得笔墨难描。我自己也够难受的——这不必细说——几秒钟里，我摔了个身高加倍的长跤；可说实话，我为这姑娘难受得也差不多，难受得除了偶尔一阵，几乎生不出她的气。她的理由是正当的：她不过是个孩子；她被置于一个不公道的境地；她若欺骗了自己、也欺骗了我，那原是意料中事。
再者，她如今也是孤苦得很。

===Original===

Her father, when he was by, was rather a caressing parent; but he was very easy led away by his affairs and pleasures, neglected her without compunction or remark, spent his nights in taverns when he had the money, which was more often than I could at all account for; and even in the course of these few days, failed once to come to a meal, which Catriona and I were at last compelled to partake of without him. It was the evening meal, and I left immediately that I had eaten, observing I supposed she would prefer to be alone; to which she agreed and (strange as it may seem) I quite believed her. Indeed, I thought myself but an eyesore to the girl, and a reminder of a moment’s weakness that she now abhorred to think of. So she must sit alone in that room where she and I had been so merry, and in the blink of that chimney whose light had shone upon our many difficult and tender moments. There she must sit alone, and think of herself as of a maid who had most unmaidenly proffered her affections and had the same rejected. And in the meanwhile I would be alone some other place, and reading myself (whenever I was tempted to be angry) lessons upon human frailty and female delicacy. And altogether I suppose there were never two poor fools made themselves more unhappy in a greater misconception.
As for James, he paid not so much heed to us, or to anything in nature but his pocket, and his belly, and his own prating talk. Before twelve hours were gone he had raised a small loan of me; before thirty, he had asked for a second and been refused. Money and refusal he took with the same kind of high good-nature. Indeed, he had an outside air of magnanimity that was very well fitted to impose upon a daughter; and the light in which he was constantly presented in his talk, and the man’s fine presence and great ways went together pretty harmoniously. So that a man that had no business with him, and either very little penetration or a furious deal of prejudice, might almost have been taken in. To me, after my first two interviews, he was as plain as print; I saw him to be perfectly selfish, with a perfect innocency in the same; and I would harken to his swaggering talk (of arms, and “an old soldier,” and “a poor Highland gentleman,” and “the strength of my country and my friends”) as I might to the babbling of a parrot.

===Chinese===

她父亲在家时，倒还算个爱怜子女的父亲；可他极容易跟着自己的事务和享乐跑掉，撟下她既无愧色也不置一词，手里一有钱便夜宿酒馆——那钱的来路，常常是我全然解释不了的；就在这几天里，他还误了一顿饭，害得卡特琳娜和我最后只好没他先吃。那是晚饭，我一吃完便走，说我料想她宁可独自待着；她应了，而且（说来奇怪）我竟全然信了。是的，我想自己在这姑娘眼里不过是个碍眼的存在，一瞬软弱的活的提醒，而那软弱她如今想都不愿想。于是她只能独自坐在那间屋子里——她同我在那里曾那样快活——守着那座烟囱的火光，它曾照过我们多少艰难而温存的时刻。她只能独自坐在那里，把自己想成一个极不庄重地献了情又被拒收的姑娘。而与此同时，我在另一处独自待着，每逢要动气，便给自己上一课，讲讲人性的脆弱与女子的娇羞。我想，天底下恐怕再没有哪两个可怜的傻子，能在更大的误会里把自己弄得更苦了。
至于詹姆斯，他不大把我们放在心上，天地万物也不大放在心上——除了他的口袋、他的肚皮、他自己那套夸夸其谈。不出十二个钟头，他从我这里借走一小笔款子；不出三十个钟头，又开口借第二笔，被回绝了。钱和回绝，他都以同一种雍容的好脾气照单全收。的确，他有一副豪爽大度的外皮，拿来哄骗一个女儿再合适不过；他言谈里给自己不断涂刷的光彩，配上这人堂堂的仪表和大手大脚的做派，倒也相当地调和。因此，一个同他无私交、而又要么眼力极浅、要么成见极深的人，几乎要着了他的道儿。至于我，头两回交锋之后，他就白纸黑字一般明白；我看清他自私得纯粹，而且自私得全然天真；听他吹嘘（谈兵器啦，「老行伍」啦，「穷高地绅士」啦，「我祖国和朋友们的能力」啦），我就像听一只鹦鹉聒噪。

===Original===

The odd thing was that I fancy he believed some part of it himself, or did at times; I think he was so false all through that he scarce knew when he was lying; and for one thing, his moments of dejection must have been wholly genuine. There were times when he would be the most silent, affectionate, clinging creature possible, holding Catriona’s hand like a big baby, and begging of me not to leave if I had any love to him; of which, indeed, I had none, but all the more to his daughter. He would press and indeed beseech us to entertain him with our talk, a thing very difficult in the state of our relations; and again break forth in pitiable regrets for his own land and friends, or into Gaelic singing.
“This is one of the melancholy airs of my native land,” he would say. “You may think it strange to see a soldier weep, and indeed it is to make a near friend of you,” says he. “But the notes of this singing are in my blood, and the words come out of my heart. And when I mind upon my red mountains and the wild birds calling there, and the brave streams of water running down, I would scarce think shame to weep before my enemies.” Then he would sing again, and translate to me pieces of the song, with a great deal of boggling and much expressed contempt against the English language. “It says here,” he would say, “that the sun is gone down, and the battle is at an end, and the brave chiefs are defeated. And it tells here how the stars see them fleeing into strange countries or lying dead on the red mountain; and they will never more shout the call of battle or wash their feet in the streams of the valley. But if you had only some of this language, you would weep also because the words of it are beyond all expression, and it is mere mockery to tell you it in English.”
Well, I thought there was a good deal of mockery in the business, one way and another; and yet, there was some feeling too, for which I hated him, I think, the worst of all. And it used to cut me to the quick to see Catriona so much concerned for the old rogue, and weeping herself to see him weep, when I was sure one-half of his distress flowed from his last night’s drinking in some tavern.

===Chinese===

奇怪的是，我觉着他自己的话，他也有几分信，至少有时信；我想这个人从头到脚假透了，假到几乎不知何时在撒谎；只有一样：他垂头丧气的那些时刻，必是全然不假的。有些时候，他会变成一个再沉默、再缠人不过的可亲生灵，攟着卡特琳娜的手，像个大婴孩，求我若对他还有一分情意就不要走；这份情意，说实话，我对他没有，对他的女儿倒是加倍地有。他会催促——简直是恳求——我们说说话给他解闷，这在我们两人那般关系的当口实在为难；转而又会迸出一串可怜的故土故人之叹，或是唱起盖尔语的歌。
「这是我故乡一支忧郁的调子，」他会这样说。「见一个军人落泪，你们或觉得蹊跷；这实在是把你们当至亲了，」他说。「可这歌唱的调子流在我的血里，词句是从我心里出来的。一想起我那些红色的山峦、山上野鸟的啼唤、奔腾而下的壮丽流水，就是当着仇人，我落泪也不觉得羞。」然后他会再唱，把歌词一段段翻给我听，翻得磕磕绊绊，还对英语大表轻蔑。「这里唱的是，」他说，「太阳落了，仗打完了，英勇的酋长们败了。这里还唱，星辰看着他们逃进异邦，或者横死在红色的山上；他们再也不会喊出战斗的呼号，再也不能在山谷的溪流里洗脚了。可你但凡懂一点这门语言，你也要落泪的——那词句妙得非言语所能表达，用英语讲给你听，简直是糟蹋。」
嗐，我看这把戏里里外外做戏的成分不少；可里头也捻着几分真情——正是为这几分真情，我想我恨他恨得最狠。而眼看卡特琳娜这样牵挂这老滑头，见他落泪自己也落泪，我心头像刀割：因为我拿得稳，他的悲苦有一半是从昨夜哪家酒馆里灌出来的。

===Original===

There were times when I was tempted to lend him a round sum, and see the last of him for good; but this would have been to see the last of Catriona as well, for which I was scarcely so prepared; and besides, it went against my conscience to squander my good money on one who was so little of a husband.

===Chinese===

有几回我动了心，想索性借他一整笔钱，从此同他一刀两断，再不见面；可那样一来，卡特琳娜也要一并再见，这个我却万万没有准备；再说，把我的好钱糟蹋在这么一个全不会当家的人身上，也违背我的良心。

## XXI The Voyage Into Holland / 二十一 荊兰之行
===Original===

The ship lay at a single anchor, well outside the pier of Leith, so that all we passengers must come to it by the means of skiffs. This was very little troublesome, for the reason that the day was a flat calm, very frosty and cloudy, and with a low shifting fog upon the water. The body of the vessel was thus quite hid as I drew near, but the tall spars of her stood high and bright in a sunshine like the flickering of a fire. She proved to be a very roomy, commodious merchant, but somewhat blunt in the bows, and loaden extraordinary deep with salt, salted salmon, and fine white linen stockings for the Dutch. Upon my coming on board, the captain welcomed me, one Sang (out of Lesmahago, I believe), a very hearty, friendly tarpauling of a man, but at the moment in rather of a bustle. There had no other of the passengers yet appeared, so that I was left to walk about upon the deck, viewing the prospect and wondering a good deal what these farewells should be which I was promised.
All Edinburgh and the Pentland Hills glinted above me in a kind of smuisty brightness, now and again overcome with blots of cloud; of Leith there was no more than the tops of chimneys visible, and on the face of the water, where the haar24 lay, nothing at all. Out of this I was presently aware of a sound of oars pulling, and a little after (as if out of the smoke of a fire) a boat issued. There sat a grave man in the stern sheets, well muffled from the cold, and by his side a tall, pretty, tender figure of a maid that brought my heart to a stand. I had scarce the time to catch my breath in, and be ready to meet her, as she stepped upon the deck, smiling, and making my best bow, which was now vastly finer than some months before when I first made it to her ladyship. No doubt we were both a good deal changed; she seemed to have shot up taller, like a young, comely tree. She had now a kind of pretty backwardness that became her well, as of one that regarded herself more highly and was fairly woman; and for another thing, the hand of the same magician had been at work upon the pair of us, and Miss Grant had made us both *braw*, if she could make but the one *bonny*.
The same cry, in words not very different, came from both of us, that the other was come in compliment to say farewell, and then we perceived in a flash we were to ship together.
“O, why will not Baby have been telling me!”

===Chinese===

船在离利斯码头很远的地方下了单锚泊定，我们乘客都得靠小艇摆渡过去。这天一点也不费事，因为海面平得纹丝不动，天寒而多云，水面上浮着一层低低的流动的雾。船身因此全然隐没，可它高高的桅桁在明灭如炉火的阳光里挺立着，又高又亮。它原来是条极宽敞舒适的商船，只是船头略钝，吃水吃得异乎寻常地深，满载着盐、腌鲑鱼和运给荷兰人的精细白亚麻长袜。我上船时，船长迎接了我——他姓桑（我记得是莱斯马哈戈人），一个热诚友好的老水手，只是这会儿正忙作一团。其余乘客还没有一个露面，我便独自在甲板上踱来踱去，观望景色，心里好一阵纳闷：人家许诺我的那些送别，究竟会是怎样的光景。
整个爱丁堡和彭特兰山在我上方一种烟蒙蒙的亮光里闪烁，时时被云块遮没；利斯只剩一排烟囱顶露在外面，而海面上——雾霭24笼罩之处——更是什么也看不见。雾中，我随即听出一片摇橹声，不多时（仿佛从炉烟里钻出似的）驶出一条小船。船尾座上坐着一位神情凝重的先生，裹得严严实实御寒；他身旁是一个高挑、秀美、柔婉的少女身影，一下子叫我的心脏停了摆。我几乎来不及吸口气、定定神迎她，她已含笑踏上甲板；我行了我最漂亮的一躬——比起几个月前初次向她行礼时，这一躬漂亮得多了。不用说，我们两个都变了许多；她仿佛抽条拔高了不少，像一棵年轻挺秀的树。她如今添了一种动人的矜持，于她十分相宜，像是一个人把自个儿看得更高了，是个十足的女子了；再者，同一位魔法师的手在我们两人身上都施了工：格兰特小姐把我们两个都打扮得*漂漂亮亮*——若说只有一样她能办到，那便是叫人*标致*。
两人异口同声——字句大同小异——都道对方是特来道别的；随即我们霜时醒悟：原来要同船而行。
「嗐，宝贝怎么就不告诉我！」

===Original===

she cried; and then remembered a letter she had been given, on the condition of not opening it till she was well on board. Within was an enclosure for myself, and ran thus:

> “Dear Davie﻿—What do you think of my farewell? and what do you say to your fellow-passenger? Did you kiss, or did you ask? I was about to have signed here, but that would leave the purport of my question doubtful; and in my own case *I ken the answer*. So fill up here with good advice. Do not be too blate,25 and for God’s sake do not try to be too forward; nothing sets you worse. I am
> “Your affectionate friend and governess,
> “Barbara Grant.”

===Chinese===

她叫道；随即想起人家交给她的一封信，条件是上了船才许拆。信里附着一封给我的，写道：

> 「亲爱的戴维——你道我这送别如何？又说你这位同船的旅伴如何？你们亲了嘴没有，还是先请了示？我本想就此署名，可那样我这问话的意思便悬而未决了；至于我自己嘛，*我可是知道答案的*。所以这里就由你来填上几句金玉良言吧。别太腼腆，25千万千万，也别硬充殷勤；没有比那更折损你的了。我是
> 「你深情的朋友兼女师父
> 「芭芭拉·格兰特」

===Original===

I wrote a word of answer and compliment on a leaf out of my pocketbook, put it in with another scratch from Catriona, sealed the whole with my new signet of the Balfour arms, and despatched it by the hand of Prestongrange’s servant that still waited in my boat.
Then we had time to look upon each other more at leisure, which we had not done for a piece of a minute before (upon a common impulse) we shook hands again.
“Catriona!” said I; it seemed that was the first and last word of my eloquence.
“You will be glad to see me again?” says she.
“And I think that is an idle word,” said I. “We are too deep friends to make speech upon such trifles.”
“Is she not the girl of all the world?” she cried again. “I was never knowing such a girl, so honest and so beautiful.”
“And yet she cared no more for Alpin than what she did for a kale-stock,” said I.
“Ah, she will say so indeed!” cries Catriona. “Yet it was for the name and the gentle kind blood that she took me up and was so good to me.”
“Well, I will tell you why it was,” said I. “There are all sorts of people’s faces in this world. There is Barbara’s face, that everyone must look at and admire, and think her a fine, brave, merry girl. And then there is your face, which is quite different, I never knew how different till today. You cannot see yourself, and that is why you do not understand; but it was for the love of your face that she took you up and was so good to you. And everybody in the world would do the same.”
“Everybody?” says she.
“Every living soul!” said I.
“Ah, then, that will be why the soldiers at the castle took me up!” she cried.
“Barbara has been teaching you to catch me,” said I.
“She will have taught me more than that at all events. She will have taught me a great deal about Mr. David﻿—all the ill of him, and a little that was not so ill either now and then,” she said, smiling. “She will have told me all there was of Mr. David, only just that he would sail upon this very same ship. And why is it you go?”
I told her.
“Ah, well,” said she, “we will be some days in company and then (I suppose) goodbye for altogether! I go to meet my father at a place of the name of Helvoetsluys, and from there to France, to be exiles by the side of our chieftain.”
I could say no more than just “O!” the name of James More always drying up my very voice.

===Chinese===

我在记事本上撕下一页，写了句答谢的客气话，连同卡特琳娜涂的几笔一并装进去，用我新刻的鲍尔弗家徽印章封了口，交由仍候在我小船里的普雷斯顿格兰奇家仆人送去。
然后我们才有空细细打量彼此——方才还没打量满一分钟，两人便（凭着一股共同的冲动）又握起手来。
「卡特琳娜！」我说；这似乎便是我全部口才的头一个字，也是末一个字。
「又见到我，你高兴吧？」她说。
「这话可就多余了，」我说。「咱们交情这么深，犯不上为这点小事费口舌。」
「她岂不是天下头一号的姑娘？」她又叫道。「我从没见过这样的姑娘，这样诚实，这样美。」
「可阿尔平在她心里，还不如一棵甘蓝梗呢。」我说。
「嗐，她嘴上自然这么说！」卡特琳娜叫道。「可她肯提携我、待我那么好，为的正是那个姓氏和那份高贵血脉。」
「好吧，我来告诉你为的是什么。这世上的脸孔有千般百种。有芭芭拉那样的脸，谁见了都要看、都要赞，都说她是个俊俏、勇敢、快活的姑娘。然后还有你这张脸，全然是另一路——到今天我才知道有多不一样。你自己看不见自己，所以你不明白；可她肯提携你、待你那么好，为的正是爱你这张脸。普天之下，人人都会这么做。」
「人人？」她说。
「每一个活着的人！」我说。
「啊，那城堡里的士兵们抬举我，也是为的这个喽！」她叫道。
「芭芭拉教会你拿我打趣了。」我说。
「她教我的可不止这一样。她教了我许许多多关于戴维先生的事——全是他的坏处，偶尔也夹一两件不那么坏的。」她含笑说。「戴维先生有什么，她都讲给我听了，只瞒着一件事：他要乘的正是这一条船。你为什么去？」
我告诉了她。
「嗐，也好，」她说，「咱们同路几天，然后（我猜）就此永别了！我要去一个叫赫尔沃特斯路易斯的地方迎我父亲，然后去法兰西，到咱们酋长身边做流亡人。」
我只应得出一个「哦！」——詹姆斯·莫尔的名字，总是把我嗓子里的声音吸干。

===Original===

She was quick to perceive it, and to guess some portion of my thought.
“There is one thing I must be saying first of all, Mr. David,” said she. “I think two of my kinsfolk have not behaved to you altogether very well. And the one of them two is James More, my father, and the other is the Laird of Prestongrange. Prestongrange will have spoken by himself, or his daughter in the place of him. But for James More, my father, I have this much to say: he lay shackled in a prison; he is a plain honest soldier and a plain Highland gentleman; what they would be after, he never would be guessing; but if he had understood it was to be some prejudice to a young gentleman like yourself, he would have died first. And for the sake of all your friendships, I will be asking you to pardon my father and family for that same mistake.”
“Catriona,” said I, “what that mistake was I do not care to know. I know but the one thing, that you went to Prestongrange and begged my life upon your knees. O, I ken well it was for your father that you went, but when you were there you pleaded for me also. It is a thing I cannot speak of. There are two things I cannot think of in to myself; and the one is your good words when you called yourself my little friend, and the other that you pleaded for my life. Let us never speak more, we two, of pardon or offence.”
We stood after that silent, Catriona looking on the deck and I on her; and before there was more speech, a little wind having sprung up, in the nor’-west, they began to shake out the sails and heave in upon the anchor.
There were six passengers besides our two selves, which made of it a full cabin. Three were solid merchants out of Leith, Kirkaldy, and Dundee, all engaged in the same adventure into High Germany; one was a Hollander returning; the rest worthy merchants’ wives, to the charge of one of whom Catriona was recommended. Mrs. Grebbie (for that was her name) was by great good fortune heavily incommoded by the sea, and lay day and night on the broad of her back. We were besides the only creatures at all young on board the *Rose*, except a white-faced boy that did my old duty to attend upon the table; and it came about that Catriona and I were left almost entirely to ourselves. We had the next seats together at the table, where I waited on her with extraordinary pleasure.

===Chinese===

她立刻察觉了，也猜中了我心思的一角。
「有一件事我得先说，戴维先生，」她说。「我想我有两位亲眷，待你实在算不得好。一位是詹姆斯·莫尔，我父亲；另一位是普雷斯顿格兰奇老爷。普雷斯顿格兰奇的话，他自会亲自说，或者由他女儿代说。至于詹姆斯·莫尔，我父亲，我要说的就这么几句：他那时戴着镣铐躺在监牢里；他是个直来直去的诚实军人，一个直来直去的高地绅士；他们葫芦里卖的什么药，他万万猜不着；可他若是明白那要害一位像你这样的年轻绅士，他宁可先死。看在咱们全部交情的份上，我求你宽恕我父亲和族人那一场过错。」
「卡特琳娜，」我说，「那过错究竟是什么，我不想知道。我只知道一件事：你去到普雷斯顿格兰奇那里，跪着乞求我一条命。嗐，我清楚你是为父亲去的，可你到了那里，也替我求了情。这是我说不出口的事。有两件事，我自己独处时连想都不敢想：一件是你自称我小朋友时的那些好话，另一件是你为我乞命。咱们两个，往后谁也别再提宽恕或冒犯了。」
此后我们默默站着，卡特琳娜望着甲板，我望着她；还没再开口，西北方向起了点小风，水手们开始抖帆张帆、绞收锚链。
除我们两人外还有六名乘客，把船舱坐得满满当当。三位是利斯、柯科迪和邓迪来的殷实商人，全做的是同一趟上德意志的生意；一位是回国的荷兰人；其余是几位体面的商人太太——卡特琳娜便托付给其中一位照管。格雷比太太（她叫这个名姓）托天之福，晕船晕得厉害，日夜仰面躺着。「玫瑰号」上除我们之外，年轻的只有那个伺候餐桌的白脸少年，干的正是我当年的旧差使；于是乎，卡特琳娜同我几乎全然只剩彼此作伴。餐桌旁我们的座位紧挨着，我在那里伺候她，快活得非同寻常。

===Original===

On deck, I made her a soft place with my cloak; and the weather being singularly fine for that season, with bright frosty days and nights, a steady, gentle wind, and scarce a sheet started all the way through the North Sea, we sat there (only now and again walking to and fro for warmth) from the first blink of the sun till eight or nine at night under the clear stars. The merchants or Captain Sang would sometimes glance and smile upon us, or pass a merry word or two and give us the go-by again; but the most part of the time they were deep in herring and chintzes and linen, or in computations of the slowness of the passage, and left us to our own concerns, which were very little important to any but ourselves.
At the first, we had a great deal to say, and thought ourselves pretty witty; and I was at a little pains to be the beau, and she (I believe) to play the young lady of experience. But soon we grew plainer with each other; I laid aside my high, clipped English (what little there was of it) and forgot to make my Edinburgh bows and scrapes; she upon her side, fell into a sort of kind familiarity; and we dwelt together like those of the same household, only (upon my side) with a more deep emotion. About the same time, the bottom seemed to fall out of our conversation, and neither one of us the less pleased. Whiles she would tell me old wives’ tales, of which she had a wonderful variety, many of them from my friend redheaded Niel. She told them very pretty, and they were pretty enough childish tales; but the pleasure to myself was in the sound of her voice, and the thought that she was telling and I listening. Whiles, again, we would sit entirely silent, not communicating even with a look, and tasting pleasure enough in the sweetness of that neighbourhood. I speak here only for myself. Of what was in the maid’s mind, I am not very sure that ever I asked myself; and what was in my own, I was afraid to consider. I need make no secret of it now, either to myself or to the reader: I was fallen totally in love. She came between me and the sun. She had grown suddenly taller, as I say, but with a wholesome growth; she seemed all health, and lightness, and brave spirits; and I thought she walked like a young deer, and stood like a birch upon the mountains.

===Chinese===

甲板上，我用自己的斗篷给她铺了个软座；那时节的天气出奇地好，昼夜里霜天明亮，风稳而柔，一路穿过北海几乎没有一张帆需要收放；我们便坐在那里（只偶尔起身来回走走取暖），从太阳头一眼眨眼坐到夜里八九点、头上满是清亮的星。商人们或桑船长时不时朝我们瞥一眼、笑笑，或者丢下一两句打趣话又走开；但大部分时间，他们都深深埋在鲱鱼、印花布和亚麻布里，或埋头计算这航程何其迟缓，把我们留给自己的事——那些除了我们俩、于谁都无足轻重的事。
起初，我们有说不完的话，自觉妙语连珠；我略费了些心思扮演时髦青年，她（我相信）则扮演见过世面的小姐。可很快我们便彼此素朴起来；我掠下了那口矜持的、拑头去尾的英语（本来也没多少），忘了行爱丁堡式的鞠躬作揖；她那一边，也落入一种亲厚的家常；我们相处得如同一家人，只是（在我这一边）情意更深一层。大约与此同时，我们的话头仿佛忽然见了底，可两人谁也不觉扫兴。她时不时给我讲些老妈妈的古经，她肚里的存货五花八门，许多还是从我那位红脑袋尼尔朋友那里来的。她讲得极好听，故事本身也不过是些孩子气的玩意；可我的乐趣在于她嗓音的声响，在于她讲着我听着这一想。有时我们又全然静坐，连眼色也不通一个，单在相近相守的甜里尝够快活。这里我只替自己说话。姑娘心里装着什么，我不记得自己可曾问过；我自己心里装着什么，我又不敢深究。如今这层纸对谁也不必捅破了，无论对我自己还是对读者：我整个儿恋爱了。她隔在我同太阳之间。她像我说过的，忽然长高了，可长得康健；她周身是健康、轻盈和一股英气；我觉得她走起来像头小鹿，立起来像山上一株白桦。

===Original===

It was enough for me to sit near by her on the deck; and I declare I scarce spent two thoughts upon the future, and was so well content with what I then enjoyed that I was never at the pains to imagine any further step; unless perhaps that I would be sometimes tempted to take her hand in mine and hold it there. But I was too like a miser of what joys I had and would venture nothing on a hazard.
What we spoke was usually of ourselves or of each other, so that if anyone had been at so much pains as overhear us, he must have supposed us the most egotistical persons in the world. It befell one day when we were at this practice, that we came on a discourse of friends and friendship, and I think now that we were sailing near the wind. We said what a fine thing friendship was, and how little we had guessed of it, and how it made life a new thing, and a thousand covered things of the same kind that will have been said, since the foundation of the world, by young folk in the same predicament. Then we remarked upon the strangeness of that circumstance, that friends came together in the beginning as if they were there for the first time, and yet each had been alive a good while, losing time with other people.
“It is not much that I have done,” said she, “and I could be telling you the five-fifths of it in two-three words. It is only a girl I am, and what can befall a girl, at all events? But I went with the clan in the year ’45. The men marched with swords and firelocks, and some of them in brigades in the same set of tartan; they were not backward at the marching, I can tell you. And there were gentlemen from the Low Country, with their tenants mounted and trumpets to sound, and there was a grand skirling of war-pipes. I rode on a little Highland horse on the right hand of my father, James More, and of Glengyle himself. And here is one fine thing that I remember, that Glengyle kissed me in the face, because (says he) ‘my kinswoman, you are the only lady of the clan that has come out,’ and me a little maid of maybe twelve years old! I saw Prince Charlie too, and the blue eyes of him; he was pretty indeed! I had his hand to kiss in the front of the army. O, well, these were the good days, but it is all like a dream that I have seen and then awakened.

===Chinese===

在甲板上坐在她身旁，于我便已足够；我声明，我几乎没为将来费过两个念头，眼前的福分已叫我心满意足，从没劳神去想再往前走一步；顶多有时候心痒，想把她的手握在我手里，就那么握着。可我对到手的欢愉太像个守财奴，什么险也不肯冒。
我们谈的通常不外我们自己或彼此，倘若有人费那么大劲偷听，必当我们是天下最自我中心的两个人。有一天正这么谈着，我们谈到了朋友和友谊；如今回想，我们那时是在贴着风的边缘航行。我们说友谊是何等妙的东西，说我们从前对它何等一无所知，说它把生命变成了崭新的一样东西，还有千句万句遮遮掩掩的同类话——自开天辟地以来，凡处在同样境地的年轻人，怕都已说过千遍。随后我们又感叹一桩怪事：朋友初聚时，仿佛彼此都是头一回来到世上，可其实两人都已活了不短的日子，把光阴虚掷在旁人身上。
「我这一辈子经过的事不多，」她说，「两三句话就能给你讲掉五分之五。我不过是个姑娘，一个姑娘能遇上什么呢？可四五年那阵子，我是跟着宗族走的。男人们佩刀拑枪行军，有几队人马穿着同一色的格子呢；他们行军可不含糊，这话我可告诉你。还有低地来的绅士们，佃户们骑着马，号角齐鸣，战笛吹得震天响。我骑一匹高地小马，走在我父亲詹姆斯·莫尔和格伦盖尔本人的右手边。有一件妙事我记得真切：格伦盖尔亲了我的脸，因为（他说）『我的侄孙女，你是全氏族唯一出阵的女眷』——而我不过是个十二岁上下的小丫头！我还见过查理王子，见过他那双蓝眼睛；他真是俊！在千军万马之前，我吻过他的手。嗐，那都是好日子，可如今全像一场梦，梦见过，又醒了。

===Original===

It went what way you very well know; and these were the worst days of all, when the redcoat soldiers were out, and my father and my uncles lay in the hill, and I was to be carrying them their meat in the middle night, or at the short side of day when the cocks crow. Yes, I have walked in the night, many’s the time, and my heart great in me for terror of the darkness. It is a strange thing I will never have been meddled with a bogle; but they say a maid goes safe. Next there was my uncle’s marriage, and that was a dreadful affair beyond all. Jean Kay was that woman’s name; and she had me in the room with her that night at Inversnaid, the night we took her from her friends in the old, ancient manner. She would and she wouldn’t; she was for marrying Rob the one minute, and the next she would be for none of him. I will never have seen such a feckless creature of a woman; surely all there was of her would tell her ay or no. Well, she was a widow, and I can never be thinking a widow a good woman.”
“Catriona!” says I, “how do you make out that?”
“I do not know,” said she; “I am only telling you the seeming in my heart. And then to marry a new man! Fy! But that was her; and she was married again upon my Uncle Robin, and went with him awhile to kirk and market; and then wearied, or else her friends got claught of her and talked her round, or maybe she turned ashamed; at the least of it, she ran away, and went back to her own folk, and said we had held her in the lake, and I will never tell you all what. I have never thought much of any females since that day. And so in the end my father, James More, came to be cast in prison, and you know the rest of it as well as me.”
“And through all you had no friends?” said I.
“No,” said she; “I have been pretty chief with two-three lasses on the braes, but not to call it friends.”
“Well, mine is a plain tale,” said I. “I never had a friend to my name till I met in with you.”
“And that brave Mr. Stewart?” she asked.
“O, yes, I was forgetting him,” I said. “But he is a man, and that is very different.”
“I would think so,” said she. “O, yes, it is quite different.”
“And then there was one other,” said I. “I once thought I had a friend, but it proved a disappointment.”
She asked me who she was?
“It was a he, then,” said I. “We were the two best lads at my father’s school, and we thought we loved each other dearly.

===Chinese===

「后来事怎么变的，你清楚得很；那才是最苦的日子：红衣兵四出搜山，我父亲和几位伯叔藏在山里，我半夜里、或是鸡叫时分天蒙蒙亮，给他们送饭。是了，夜里赶路我走过多少回，心口揣着老大一团对黑地的恐惧。说来奇怪，我竟从没撞见过鬼；人家说，姑娘走夜路，百无禁忌。再往后就是我伯父的婚事，那可是桩天字第一号的可怕事。那女人名叫琼·凯；在因弗斯奈德那一夜——我们按老祖宗的规矩把她从她亲友手里抢来的那一夜——她把我留在她房里。她一会儿肯一会儿不肯；这一刻要嫁罗布，下一刻又死活不要他。这样没主见的女人我从未见过；她浑身上下，竟没有一处能替她拿个准主意。嗐，她是个寡妇，而我无论如何不能把寡妇当好人。」
「卡特琳娜！」我说，「这话怎么讲？」
「我不知道，」她说；「我不过把心里的成见说给你听。再说，还要再嫁一个新男人！呸！她就是那样的人；她嫁给了我罗宾伯父，跟他上了一段教堂、赶了一段集市；然后就腻了，要么是她娘家亲友逮住她、把她劝转了，要么是她自己害了臊；到头来，她跑了，回娘家人去了，还说是我们把她扣在湖里，还有种种我不愿全告诉你的话。打那天起，我对一切女人都不大看得起了。再后来，我父亲詹姆斯·莫尔下了大狱，底下的事你同我一样清楚。」
「这一路过来，你一个朋友也没有？」我说。
「没有，」她说；「山坡上有两三个姑娘同我要好，可还算不得朋友。」
「嗐，我的故事也简单，」我说。「认识你之前，我名下从没有过一个朋友。」
「那位了不起的斯图尔特先生呢？」她问。
「哦，对，我把他忘了，」我说。「可他是个男人，那全然是另一回事。」
「我也这么想，」她说。「哦，是，全然不同。」
「另外还有过一个，」我说。「我一度以为有了个朋友，结果大失所望。」
她问我那是谁。
「那是个男的，」我说。「我们是我父亲学堂里最出挑的两个少年，我们以为自己好得不得了。

===Original===

Well, the time came when he went to Glasgow to a merchant’s house, that was his second cousin once removed; and wrote me two-three times by the carrier; and then he found new friends, and I might write till I was tired, he took no notice. Eh, Catriona, it took me a long while to forgive the world. There is not anything more bitter than to lose a fancied friend.”
Then she began to question me close upon his looks and character, for we were each a great deal concerned in all that touched the other; till at last, in a very evil hour, I minded of his letters and went and fetched the bundle from the cabin.
“Here are his letters,” said I, “and all the letters that ever I got. That will be the last I’ll can tell of myself; you know the lave26 as well as I do.”
“Will you let me read them, then?” says she.
I told her, *if she would be at the pains*; and she bade me go away and she would read them from the one end to the other. Now, in this bundle that I gave her, there were packed together not only all the letters of my false friend, but one or two of Mr. Campbell’s when he was in town at the Assembly, and to make a complete roll of all that ever was written to me, Catriona’s little word, and the two I had received from Miss Grant, one when I was on the Bass and one on board that ship. But of these last I had no particular mind at the moment.
I was in that state of subjection to the thought of my friend that it mattered not what I did, nor scarce whether I was in her presence or out of it; I had caught her like some kind of a noble fever that lived continually in my bosom, by night and by day, and whether I was waking or asleep. So it befell that after I was come into the forepart of the ship where the broad bows splashed into the billows, I was in no such hurry to return as you might fancy; rather prolonged my absence like a variety in pleasure. I do not think I am by nature much of an Epicurean; and there had come till then so small a share of pleasure in my way that I might be excused perhaps to dwell on it unduly.
When I returned to her again, I had a faint, painful impression as of a buckle slipped, so coldly she returned the packet.
“You have read them?” said I; and I thought my voice sounded not wholly natural, for I was turning in my mind for what could ail her.
“Did you mean me to read all?” she asked.
I told her “Yes,” with a drooping voice.

===Chinese===

后来时辰到了，他去格拉斯哥一家商号学生意——东家是他隔房的远房表亲；起初还托赶车的捎过两三封信；后来他交了新朋友，我写信写得手软，他也不理了。嗐，卡特琳娜，我花了好长时间才肯饬恕这个世界。再没有比失去一个意想中的朋友更苦的事了。」
接着她便细细盘问起他的相貌品性来——凡牵动对方的事，我们彼此都关切得很；直到最后，在那个倒霉透顶的时辰，我想起了他的信，便去舱里把那一捆取了来。
「这是他的信，还有我此生收到的所有的信。我自己的事，能讲的到这就讲完了；其余的26你同我一样清楚。」
「那能让我读一读么？」她说。
我告诉她，*只要她肯费这个神*；她便叫我走开，说她要从头一封读到末一封。而我递给她的这一捆里，收的不光是我那负心朋友的全部来信，还有坎贝尔先生进城开大会时写的一两封；为凑成「我此生所收信件」的全卷，还有卡特琳娜那封小简，以及格兰特小姐写来的两封——一封是我在巴斯岩上收的，一封就在这条船上。可这最后几封，我当时压根没往心里去。
我整个人沉浸在对我的姑娘的思念里，置于此般俯首帖耳的状态，做什么都无妨，在不在她眼前也几乎无妨；我染上她，像染上一种高贵的热病，日夜住在我胸中，醒着睡着都烧着。于是，走到船头那宽宽的船艏劈开波浪的地方之后，我竟不像你想的那样急着回去，倒把这离开的时光拉长，当作一种别样的享受。我自认天性不算什么伊壁鸠鲁信徒；可到那时为止，落到我路上的甜头实在少得可怜，在这一点上流连过久，或许也情有可原。
等我再回到她那里，心头掠过一丝隐约而发疼的预感，像带扣松脱了一扣——她递还那捆信时，冷得那样。
「都读完了？」我说；我觉出自己的嗓音不太自然，因为我正在心里捉摸她这是怎么了。
「你是要我全读么？」她问。
我有气无力地答了声「是」。

===Original===

“The last of them as well?” said she.
I knew where we were now; yet I would not lie to her either. “I gave them all without afterthought,” I said, “as I supposed that you would read them. I see no harm in any.”
“I will be differently made,” said she. “I thank God I am differently made. It was not a fit letter to be shown me. It was not fit to be written.”
“I think you are speaking of your own friend, Barbara Grant?” said I.
“There will not be anything as bitter as to lose a fancied friend,” said she, quoting my own expression.
“I think it is sometimes the friendship that was fancied!” I cried. “What kind of justice do you call this, to blame me for some words that a tomfool of a madcap lass has written down upon a piece of paper? You know yourself with what respect I have behaved﻿—and would do always.”
“Yet you would show me that same letter!” says she. “I want no such friends. I can be doing very well, Mr. Balfour, without her﻿—or you.”
“This is your fine gratitude!” says I.
“I am very much obliged to you,” said she. “I will be asking you to take away your﻿—letters.” She seemed to choke upon the word, so that it sounded like an oath.
“You shall never ask twice,” said I; picked up that bundle, walked a little way forward and cast them as far as possible into the sea. For a very little more, I could have cast myself after them.
The rest of the day I walked up and down raging. There were few names so ill but what I gave her them in my own mind before the sun went down. All that I had ever heard of Highland pride seemed quite outdone; that a girl (scarce grown) should resent so trifling an allusion, and that from her next friend, that she had near wearied me with praising of! I had bitter, sharp, hard thoughts of her, like an angry boy’s. If I had kissed her indeed (I thought), perhaps she would have taken it pretty well; and only because it had been written down, and with a spice of jocularity, up she must fuff in this ridiculous passion. It seemed to me there was a want of penetration in the female sex, to make angels weep over the case of the poor men.
We were side by side again at supper, and what a change was there! She was like curdled milk to me; her face was like a wooden doll’s; I could have indifferently smitten her or grovelled at her feet, but she gave me not the least occasion to do either.

===Chinese===

「最后那几封也读？」她说。
我明白眼下我们站在哪儿了；可我也不肯对她撒谎。「我交出去时没有多想，」我说，「原就料到你会读的。哪一封我都看不出坏处。」
「我生得同你不一样，」她说。「感谢上帝，我生得同你不一样。那封信不该拿给我看。它压根不该写出来。」
「你说的莫不是你自己的朋友，芭芭拉·格兰特？」我说。
「再没有比失去一个意想中的朋友更苦的了。」她说，把我自己的话原样奉还。
「我看有时候，是那友谊本就出于意想！」我叫道。「这算哪门子公道——一个疯疯癫癫的傻丫头在纸片上写了几句话，倒来怪我？你自己知道我平日行止何等敬重——往后也永远如此。」
「可你竟把那样一封信拿给我看！」她说。「这样的朋友我不要。没有她——或没有你，鲍尔弗先生，我都过得很好。」
「这就是你那漂亮的知恩图报！」我说。
「真是多谢你了，」她说。「请把你的——信拿走。」说到那个字时她像噎了一下，以致那字出口，听着像句咒骂。
「这个不消你问第二遍，」我说；抓起那捆信，朝前走了几步，把它们尽力远远抛进海里。再差一点点，我就要连自己也跟着抛下去。
那天余下的时光，我来回踱步，怒火中烧。太阳落山之前，凡是不太难听的称呼，我在心里都给她安过了。我平生所闻的高地傲气，同这一比全不算数：一个（尚未长成的）姑娘，竟为这样一星半点的影射动这么大的气——还是出自她仅次于我最好的朋友、她夸得我耳朵起茧的那位之口！我对她的念头又苦、又尖、又硬，像个赌气的男孩。我若当真亲了她（我想），她说不定还受用些；只因为那话写在了纸上、带了几分玩笑，她就非得鼓起这么一场荒唐的无名火不可。依我看，女性这一族缺了点透视力——足叫天使们为可怜男子的处境一掬清泪。
晚饭时我们又并肩坐了，可一切都变了样！她于我像一杯凝结的奶；那张脸像木头娃娃的脸；我满可以不管三七二十一给她一巴掌，也可以五体投地跪倒在她脚边，可她半点机会也不给我。

===Original===

No sooner the meal done than she betook herself to attend on Mrs. Gebbie, which I think she had a little neglected heretofore. But she was to make up for lost time, and in what remained of the passage was extraordinary assiduous with the old lady, and on deck began to make a great deal more than I thought wise of Captain Sang. Not but what the captain seemed a worthy, fatherly man; but I hated to behold her in the least familiarity with anyone except myself.
Altogether, she was so quick to avoid me, and so constant to keep herself surrounded with others, that I must watch a long while before I could find my opportunity; and after it was found, I made not much of it, as you are now to hear.
“I have no guess how I have offended,” said I; “it should scarce be beyond pardon, then. O, try if you can pardon me.”
“I have no pardon to give,” said she; and the words seemed to come out of her throat like marbles. “I will be very much obliged for all your friendships.” And she made me an eight part of a curtsey.
But I had schooled myself beforehand to say more, and I was going to say it too.
“There is one thing,” said I. “If I have shocked your particularity by the showing of that letter, it cannot touch Miss Grant. She wrote not to you, but to a poor, common, ordinary lad, who might have had more sense than show it. If you are to blame me﻿—”
“I will advise you to say no more about that girl, at all events!” said Catriona. “It is her I will never look the road of, not if she lay dying.” She turned away from me, and suddenly back. “Will you swear you will have no more to deal with her?” she cried.
“Indeed, and I will never be so unjust then,” said I; “nor yet so ungrateful.”
And now it was I that turned away.

===Chinese===

饭一吃完，她便一头扎去照应格比太太——这一节我想她此前是有些疏忽的。她这是要补回荒废的时辰：航程剩下的日子里，她待那位老太太殷勤得出奇；在甲板上，同桑船长的热络也远过了我认为明智的限度。倒不是船长不像个正派的父辈人；可我见她对除我之外的任何人有半分亲近，心里就恨。
总之，她躲我躲得那样快，把自己裹在人堆里裹得那样牢，我守了许久才逮着机会；逮着之后又没讨到什么好，这话你听我说下去。
「我猜不出我怎么冒犯了您；想必还不至于赦不得吧。嗐，试试看，能不能饶我这一遭。」
「我没什么可饶的，」她说；那几个字像几颗弹珠从喉咙里滚出来。「你这些友谊，我领受不尽。」说着朝我行了个八分之一屈膝礼。
可我事先给自己操练过下文，而且说什么也要说完。
「有一件事，」我说。「倘若亮那封信触犯了您的清规，那也牵不到格兰特小姐头上。她不是写给你的，是写给一个又穷又平常、普普通通的小子的——那小子本该多点脑子，不去显摆它。您要怪就怪——」
「我劝你一句，那姑娘的事，往后一个字也别再提！」卡特琳娜说。「她那条路，我死也不会看一眼——哪怕她躺了咽气。」她从我身边转开，又猛地转回来。「你起誓，往后不同她再有来往，成不成？」她叫道。
「不成，那样既不公道，」我说，「也不知恩。」
这回，转开身的是我。

## XI The Wood by Silvermills / 十一 银磨坊边的树林
===Original===

I lost no time, but down through the valley and by Stockbrig and Silvermills as hard as I could stave. It was Alan’s tryst to lie every night between twelve and two “in a bit scrog of wood by east of Silvermills and by south the south mill-lade.” This I found easy enough, where it grew on a steep brae, with the mill-lade flowing swift and deep along the foot of it; and here I began to walk slower and to reflect more reasonably on my employment. I saw I had made but a fool’s bargain with Catriona. It was not to be supposed that Neil was sent alone upon his errand, but perhaps he was the only man belonging to James More; in which case, I should have done all I could to hang Catriona’s father, and nothing the least material to help myself. To tell the truth, I fancied neither one of these ideas. Suppose, by holding back Neil, the girl should have helped to hang her father, I thought she would never forgive herself this side of time. And suppose there were others pursuing me that moment, what kind of a gift was I come bringing to Alan? and how would I like that?
I was up with the west end of that wood when these two considerations struck me like a cudgel. My feet stopped of themselves and my heart along with them. “What wild game is this that I have been playing?” thought I; and turned instantly upon my heels to go elsewhere.
This brought my face to Silvermills; the path came past the village with a crook, but all plainly visible; and, Highland or Lowland, there was nobody stirring. Here was my advantage, here was just such a conjuncture as Stewart had counselled me to profit by, and I ran by the side of the mill-lade, fetched about beyond the east corner of the wood, threaded through the midst of it, and returned to the west selvage, whence I could again command the path, and yet be myself unseen. Again it was all empty, and my heart began to rise.
For more than an hour I sat close in the border of the trees, and no hare or eagle could have kept a more particular watch. When that hour began the sun was already set, but the sky still all golden and the daylight clear; before the hour was done it had fallen to be half mirk, the images and distances of things were mingled, and observation began to be difficult.

===Chinese===

我一刻也不耽搁，奔下河谷，取道斯托克布里奇和银磨坊，脚下能跑多快跑多快。艾伦的约定是每夜十二点到两点之间，「躺在银磨坊以东、南磨坊水渠以南的一小片灌木林里」。那地方很好找，长在一面陡坡上，磨坊水渠又急又深地贴着坡脚流；走到这里，我放慢了脚步，也把自己这趟差事掂量得更清醒了些。我明白，我同卡特琳娜做的实在是一桩傻瓜买卖。不能设想尼尔办这差事是单枪匹马来的；可万一他真是詹姆斯·莫尔手下仅有的人手呢？那样的话，我便等于竭尽所能把卡特琳娜的父亲送上了绞架，而于自救没有半点实益。说实话，这两样想头我都打心底里发怵。倘若扣住尼尔这一下，叫那姑娘帮着绞死了自己的父亲，我想她这辈子再不会饶恕自己。又倘若此刻还有旁的人在追我，我这算给艾伦捎来一件什么样的「礼物」？我自己乐意领受这样的礼物么？
走到树林西头，这两桩思量像短棍一样砸中了我。我的脚自己停了，心也跟着停了。「我玩的这算什么胡闹把戏？」我想着，当即转身要往别处去。
这一转身，脸正对着银磨坊；小路拤个弯擦村而过，却望得一清二楚；无论高地人低地人，一个人影也没有。我的机会来了——这正是斯图尔特指点我加以利用的那种局面。我沿着水渠一路跑，绕过树林东角兜了个圈，从林子当中穿过去，又折回西缘；在那里，小路重新落在我眼底，我自家却藏得严实。路上依旧空空如也，我的心开始往上提了。
有一个多钟头，我贴着林缘死守，兔子也好，鹰隼也好，再没有比我更精细的守望了。那钟点开头时，太阳已经落了，天却还一片金黄，天光清亮；等钟点将尽，天色已沉成半黑，万物的影子和远近都糊在了一处，观望开始吃力了。

===Original===

All that time not a foot of man had come east from Silvermills, and the few that had gone west were honest countryfolk and their wives upon the road to bed. If I were tracked by the most cunning spies in Europe, I judged it was beyond the course of nature they could have any jealousy of where I was; and going a little further home into the wood I lay down to wait for Alan.
The strain of my attention had been great, for I had watched not the path only, but every bush and field within my vision. That was now at an end. The moon, which was in her first quarter, glinted a little in the wood; all round there was a stillness of the country; and as I lay there on my back, the next three or four hours, I had a fine occasion to review my conduct.
Two things became plain to me first: that I had had no right to go that day to Dean, and (having gone there) had now no right to be lying where I was. This (where Alan was to come) was just the one wood in all broad Scotland that was, by every proper feeling, closed against me; I admitted that, and yet stayed on, wondering at myself. I thought of the measure with which I had meted to Catriona that same night; how I had prated of the two lives I carried, and had thus forced her to enjeopardy her father’s; and how I was here exposing them again, it seemed in wantonness. A good conscience is eight parts of courage. No sooner had I lost conceit of my behaviour, than I seemed to stand disarmed amidst a throng of terrors. Of a sudden I sat up. How if I went now to Prestongrange, caught him (as I still easily might) before he slept, and made a full submission? Who could blame me? Not Stewart the writer; I had but to say that I was followed, despaired of getting clear, and so gave in. Not Catriona: here, too, I had my answer ready; that I could not bear she should expose her father. So, in a moment, I could lay all these troubles by, which were after all and truly none of mine; swim clear of the Appin murder; get forth out of handstroke of all the Stewarts and Campbells, all the whigs and tories, in the land; and live thenceforth to my own mind, and be able to enjoy and to improve my fortunes, and devote some hours of my youth to courting Catriona, which would be surely a more suitable occupation than to hide and run and be followed like a hunted thief, and begin over again the dreadful miseries of my escape with Alan.

===Chinese===

这整段时间，没有一个人影从银磨坊朝东来；往西去的寥寥几个，也是赶着回床上去的老实乡民和他们的婆娘。就算全欧洲最狡黠的暗探在盯我的梢，我断定依着常理，他们也猜疑不到我的所在；我往林子深处挪了挪，躺下等艾伦。
方才那份专注绷得极紧，因为我盯的不光是小路，还有视野里的每一丛灌木、每一块田。如今这差事完了。上弦月在林中洒着微光；四野是一片乡下的寂静；我仰面躺在那里，往后三四个钟头，正好从容检点自己的行止。
有两件事先成了明摆着的：一，我今天本没有权利去迪安；二，（既去了）此刻更没有权利躺在这里。这地方是艾伦要来的——在整个幅员辽阔的苏格兰，恰恰是这一片树林，论一切正当的情理，都该对我关闭；我认这个理，却还赖着不走，自己都觉得奇怪。我想起当晚我量给卡特琳娜的那杆尺：我怎样夸夸其谈自己驮着的两条人命，又怎样逼得她拿她父亲的性命去冒险；而如今我又在这里把他们重新暴露一回，简直是无端寻祸。问心无愧，勇气便有了八成。我一失去对自己行止的敬意，便仿佛被解了械，站在一大群恐惧中间。我突然坐起身来。要是这会儿我去普雷斯顿格兰奇那里，趁他还没睡（我照旧容易办到）逮住他，来个全套的降服，如何？谁能怪我？代书人斯图尔特不能：我只需说，我被盯上了，脱身无望，只得认输。卡特琳娜也不能：这里我同样备好了答话——我不忍见她暴露她的父亲。这样，一转眼我便可以把这些烦恼统统掠下——它们说到底、实实在在原不是我份内的；从阿平杀人案里干净脱身；跳出全苏格兰斯图尔特家同坎贝尔家、辉格党同托利党所有人的打击半径；从此过自己想过的日子，坐享并增值我的家产，把青年光阴拨出几许来向卡特琳娜求爱——这差使，肯定比东躲西藏、被人像贼一样追拿、把同艾伦逃亡的那番可怕苦难从头再熬一遍，更配得上我。

===Original===

At first I thought no shame of this capitulation; I was only amazed I had not thought upon the thing and done it earlier; and began to inquire into the causes of the change. These I traced to my lowness of spirits, that back to my late recklessness, and that again to the common, old, public, disconsidered sin of self-indulgence. Instantly the text came in my head, “How can Satan cast out Satan?” What? (I thought) I had, by self-indulgence, and the following of pleasant paths, and the lure of a young maid, cast myself wholly out of conceit with my own character, and jeopardised the lives of James and Alan? And I was to seek the way out by the same road as I had entered in? No; the hurt that had been caused by self-indulgence must be cured by self-denial; the flesh I had pampered must be crucified. I looked about me for that course which I least liked to follow: this was to leave the wood without waiting to see Alan, and go forth again alone, in the dark and in the midst of my perplexed and dangerous fortunes.
I have been the more careful to narrate this passage of my reflections, because I think it is of some utility, and may serve as an example to young men. But there is reason (they say) in planting kale, and even in ethic and religion, room for common sense. It was already close on Alan’s hour, and the moon was down. If I left (as I could not very decently whistle to my spies to follow me) they might miss me in the dark and tack themselves to Alan by mistake. If I stayed, I could at the least of it set my friend upon his guard which might prove his mere salvation. I had adventured other peoples’ safety in a course of self-indulgence; to have endangered them again, and now on a mere design of penance, would have been scarce rational. Accordingly, I had scarce risen from my place ere I sat down again, but already in a different frame of spirits, and equally marvelling at my past weakness and rejoicing in my present composure.
Presently after came a crackling in the thicket. Putting my mouth near down to the ground, I whistled a note or two of Alan’s air; an answer came, in the like guarded tone, and soon we had thralled together in the dark.
“Is this you at last, Davie?” he whispered.
“Just myself,” said I.
“God, man, but I’ve been wearying to see ye!” says he. “I’ve had the longest kind of a time.

===Chinese===

起初，这投降的念头并不使我羞愧；我只奇怪自己早没想到、早没照办；便着手追查这一变化的根由。追来追去，追到我的情绪低落；再往回追，追到我近日的孟浪；再往回追，追到那桩人人皆犯、古已有之、从不被放在眼里的罪——放纵私欲。登时一句经文撞进我脑子：「撒但怎能赶出撒但呢？」什么？（我想）我因放纵私欲、贪走快活路、又受了年轻姑娘的引诱，把自己的人品败坏殆尽，还把詹姆斯同艾伦的性命押上了险地；如今倒要循着进来的原路寻出去？不；放纵招的伤，须用克己来医；我娇养惯的肉体，须钉上十字架。我环顾四周，专捫那条我最不愿走的路：那便是不同艾伦见面便出林子去，重新孤身一人，摸着黑，走进我那重重险恶的命运里。
我之所以细细记下这段心事，因为我以为它多少有点用处，或许可作年轻人的一个鉴戒。可是（常言道）种甘蓝也得讲道理，伦理和宗教里，也该给常识留个位置。已快到艾伦的时辰了，月亮也落了。我若离开（总不能体面地吹声呼哨，叫我的暗探们跟上来），他们黑里错过我，倒许错认到艾伦头上。我若留下，至少能给朋友提个醒，这说不定正是救他的命。我先前放纵私欲，拿别人的平安当了赌注；如今若为一桩苦修的计划再害他们冒险一回，就近乎荒唐了。于是，屁股才离地我又坐了回去，只是心绪已换了一副，既纳罕方才那阵软弱，又欢喜眼下这份镇定。
不多时，树丛里传来窸窣声。我把嘴凑近地面，吹了艾伦那支调子的一两个音；黑暗里传来同样压低的回应，转眼我们已在暗中聚到了一处。
「总算等到你了，戴维？」他悄声说。
「正是我。」我说。
「天哪，伙计，我盼你盼得好苦！」他说。「这段日子熬得可真叫长。

===Original===

A’ day, I’ve had my dwelling into the inside of a stack of hay, where I couldnae see the nebs of my ten fingers; and then two hours of it waiting here for you, and you never coming! Dod, and ye’re none too soon the way it is, with me to sail the morn! The morn? what am I saying?﻿—the day, I mean.”
“Ay, Alan, man, the day, sure enough,” said I. “It’s past twelve now, surely, and ye sail the day. This’ll be a long road you have before you.”
“We’ll have a long crack of it first,” said he.
“Well, indeed, and I have a good deal it will be telling you to hear,” said I.
And I told him what behooved, making rather a jumble of it, but clear enough when done. He heard me out with very few questions, laughing here and there like a man delighted: and the sound of his laughing (above all there, in the dark, where neither one of us could see the other) was extraordinary friendly to my heart.
“Ay, Davie, ye’re a queer character,” says he, when I had done: “a queer bitch after a’, and I have no mind of meeting with the like of ye. As for your story, Prestongrange is a Whig like yoursel’, so I’ll say the less of him; and, dod! I believe he was the best friend ye had, if ye could only trust him. But Symon Fraser and James More are my ain kind of cattle, and I’ll give them the name that they deserve. The muckle black de’il was father to the Frasers, a’body kens that; and as for the Gregara, I never could abye the reek of them since I could stotter on two feet. I bloodied the nose of one, I mind, when I was still so wambly on my legs that I cowped upon the top of him. A proud man was my father that day, God rest him! and I think he had the cause. I’ll never can deny but what Robin was something of a piper,” he added; “but as for James More, the de’il guide him for me!”
“One thing we have to consider,” said I. “Was Charles Stewart right or wrong? Is it only me they’re after, or the pair of us?”
“And what’s your ain opinion, you that’s a man of so much experience?” said he.
“It passes me,” said I.
“And me too,” says Alan. “Do ye think this lass would keep her word to ye?” he asked.
“I do that,” said I.
“Well, there’s nae telling,” said he. “And anyway, that’s over and done: he’ll be joined to the rest of them lang syne.”
“How many would ye think there would be of them?” I asked.
“That depends,” said Alan.

===Chinese===

一整天，我的住处就是一垛干草的肚子里头，连自己十根手指头尖都瞧不见；然后又在这儿干等你两个钟头，你愣是不来！老天，就这光景你还不算来得晚——我明天一早就开船啦！明天？我说什么呢——是今天，今天。」
「哎，艾伦，伙计，是今天，千真万确，」我说。「这会儿准过十二点了，你今天动身。你前头这条路可不近哪。」
「先容咱们痛痛快快聊个够。」他说。
「好啊，说实话，我这儿有一大堆事，正该讲给你听。」我说。
于是我把该讲的都讲了，讲得颠三倒四，讲完了倒也明白。他很少插问，一路听下来，不时像得了大乐的人那样笑；那笑声（尤其是在那样的黑暗里，你我谁也看不见谁）在我心里熨帖得非同寻常。
「嗐，戴维，你真是个怪人，」我讲完，艾伦说，「天字第一号的怪货色，我这辈子没见过第二个。至于你这段故事：普雷斯顿格兰奇是辉格党，跟你一党，他的话我少说；可老天！我相信他是你顶好的朋友——只要你肯信他。但西蒙·弗雷泽同詹姆斯·莫尔却是我们自己那路货，他们该得什么名号，我便给他们什么名号。弗雷泽家老子是那大黑魔鬼，谁人不知；至于格雷加拉家，打我能站上两条腿蹒跚走路起，就闻不惯他们那股烟味。我记得我还打过其中一个的鼻子——那会儿我腿还软着，一交跌在他身上。那天我父亲得意极了，上帝让他安息！我想他得意得有理。罗宾风笛吹得不错，这个我不否认，」他添一句；「可詹姆斯·莫尔嘛，魔鬼替我招呼他吧！」
「有一件事咱们得琢磨，」我说。「查尔斯·斯图尔特说得对不对？他们追的是我一个人，还是咱们俩？」
「你自己怎么看的，你这么个大行家？」他说。
「我想不透。」我说。
「我也想不透。」艾伦说。「你看那姑娘会对你守约么？」他问。
「会。」我说。
「嗐，这事谁说得准。」他说。「反正那一节已经完了：他早同其余那些人会合了。」
「你估他们会有多少人？」我问。
「那得看情形。」艾伦说。

===Original===

“If it was only you, they would likely send two-three lively, brisk young birkies, and if they thought that I was to appear in the employ, I daresay ten or twelve,” said he.
It was no use, I gave a little crack of laughter.
“And I think your own two eyes will have seen me drive that number, or the double of it, nearer hand!” cries he.
“It matters the less,” said I, “because I am well rid of them for this time.”
“Nae doubt that’s your opinion,” said he; “but I wouldnae be the least surprised if they were hunkering this wood. Ye see, David man, they’ll be Hieland folk. There’ll be some Frasers, I’m thinking, and some of the Gregara; and I would never deny but what the both of them, and the Gregara in especial, were clever experienced persons. A man kens little till he’s driven a spreagh of neat cattle (say) ten miles through a throng lowland country and the black soldiers maybe at his tail. It’s there that I learned a great part of my penetration. And ye need nae tell me: it’s better than war; which is the next best, however, though generally rather a bauchle of a business. Now the Gregara have had grand practice.”
“No doubt that’s a branch of education that was left out with me,” said I.
“And I can see the marks of it upon ye constantly,” said Alan. “But that’s the strange thing about you folk of the college learning: ye’re ignorant, and ye cannae see ’t. Wae’s me for my Greek and Hebrew; but, man, I ken that I dinnae ken them﻿—there’s the differ of it. Now, here’s you. Ye lie on your wame a bittie in the bield of this wood, and ye tell me that ye’ve cuist off these Frasers and Macgregors. Why! *Because I couldnae see them*, says you. Ye blockhead, that’s their livelihood.”
“Take the worst of it,” said I, “and what are we to do?”
“I am thinking of that same,” said he. “We might twine. It wouldnae be greatly to my taste; and forbye that, I see reasons against it. First, it’s now unco dark, and it’s just humanly possible we might give them the clean slip. If we keep together, we make but the ae line of it; if we gang separate, we make twae of them: the more likelihood to stave in upon some of these gentry of yours. And then, second, if they keep the track of us, it may come to a fecht for it yet, Davie; and then, I’ll confess I would be blythe to have you at my oxter, and I think you would be none the worse of having me at yours.

===Chinese===

「要是单冲你来的，他们多半派两三个活蹦乱跳的毛小子；要是他们想到我也在局里当差，我估摸着十来个、十二个。」他说。
没有法子，我噗哧笑出了一声。
「再说，亲眼看我打发掉这个数、或翻上一倍的人，你自己不就近在跟前么！」他叫道。
「好在不要紧，」我说，「这一回我已经把他们甩干净了。」
「那是你的看法，」他说；「可他们这会儿正猫在这林子里，我一点也不会吃惊。你瞧，戴维伙计，他们是高地人。我猜有弗雷泽家的，也有格雷加拉家的；这两家——尤其格雷加拉家——是精明老练的角色，这个我从不否认。一个人没赶着一群牛在熙攘的低地走上（比方说）十英里、背后兴许还吊着黑森森的兵，他就懂得少。我的眼力，一大半就是在那儿练出来的。这你不用反驳我：那比打仗还长本事；打仗算第二等——虽说通常打得很是拉垮。而格雷加拉家这一行，练得炉火纯青。」
「这一门功课，我自然是没修过。」我说。
「你身上那副没修过的样子，我可随时看得出来。」艾伦说。「不过你们这些念过大学的先生，怪就怪在这儿：明明无知，自己却看不见。我的希腊文希伯来文，唉，可怜；可伙计，我晓得我不懂它们——差别就在这儿。再看你。你在这林子的庇护里趴了一会儿肚子，就告诉我你把那些弗雷泽和麦克格雷戈甩脱了。为什么？*因为我就没见着他们*，你说。你这木头脑袋，藏形匿影就是他们的饭碗哪。」
「就依你说最坏的算，」我说，「咱们怎么办？」
「我也正琢磨这事。」他说。「咱们可以分头走。我不大乐意；何况还有几层反对的道理。第一，眼下黑得厉害，咱们未必没有干净溜掉的人间可能。待在一起，只走出一条线；分头走，就是两条：撞上你家那些大爷们的机会翻上一倍。第二，倘若他们咬住咱们的踪迹，戴维，到头来兴许还要干一仗；那我就说实话吧，有你在我胳膊底下，我欢喜；我想有我在你的胳膊底下，你也不吃亏。

===Original===

So, by my way of it, we should creep out of this wood no further gone than just the inside of next minute, and hold away east for Gillane, where I’m to find my ship. It’ll be like old days while it lasts, Davie; and (come the time) we’ll have to think what you should be doing. I’m wae to leave ye here, wanting me.”
“Have with ye, then!” says I. “Do ye gang back where you were stopping.”
“De’il a fear!” said Alan. “They were good folks to me, but I think they would be a good deal disappointed if they saw my bonny face again. For (the way times go) I amnae just what ye could call a Walcome Guest. Which makes me the keener for your company, Mr. David Balfour of the Shaws, and set ye up! For, leave aside twa cracks here in the wood with Charlie Stewart, I have scarce said black or white since the day we parted at Corstorphine.”
With which he rose from his place, and we began to move quietly eastward through the wood.

===Chinese===

「所以依我的主意，咱们该在下一分钟之内悄悄蹭出这片林子，一路朝东奔吉兰，我的船在那儿等我。这一程走多久，就多久像回到旧日子，戴维；（到时候）咱们再商量你的去处。把你一个人掠在这儿，我心里难受。」
「那就一起走！」我说。「你不回你借宿的那家去了？」
「才不回！」艾伦说。「那家人待我不坏，可我这俊脸蛋要是再露一回，他们怕要大大失望。（照如今这世道）我不大像你们说的「受欢迎的客人」。这正叫我越发离不得你作伴，肖斯的戴维·鲍尔弗先生，挺起胸来！因为，掠开同查利·斯图尔特在这林子里两回交谈不算，打咱们在科斯托芬分手那天起，我连句黑白是非的话都没同人讲过。」
说罢他从地上起身，我们便开始悄然穿林东行。

## XXIII Travels in Holland / 二十三 荷兰游历
===Original===

The rattel-wagon, which is a kind of a long wagon set with benches, carried us in four hours of travel to the great city of Rotterdam. It was long past dark by then, but the streets pretty brightly lighted and thronged with the wild-like, outlandish characters﻿—bearded Hebrews, black men, and the hordes of courtesans, most indecently adorned with finery and stopping seamen by their very sleeves; the clash of talk about us made our heads to whirl; and what was the most unexpected of all, we appeared to be no more struck with all these foreigners than they with us. I made the best face I could, for the lass’s sake and my own credit; but the truth is I felt like a lost sheep, and my heart beat in my bosom with anxiety. Once or twice I inquired after the harbor or the berth of the ship *Rose*; but either fell on some who spoke only Hollands, or my own French failed me. Trying a street at a venture, I came upon a lane of lighted houses, the doors and windows thronged with wauf-like painted women; these jostled and mocked upon us as we passed, and I was thankful we had nothing of their language. A little after we issued forth upon an open place along the harbour.
“We shall be doing now,” cries I, as soon as I spied masts. “Let us walk here by the harbour. We are sure to meet some that has the English, and at the best of it we may light upon that very ship.”
We did the next best, as happened; for about nine of the evening, whom should we walk into the arms of but Captain Sang? He told us they had made their run in the most incredible brief time, the wind holding strong until they reached port; by which means his passengers were all gone already on their further travels. It was impossible to chase after the Gebbies into High Germany, and we had no other acquaintance to fall back upon but Captain Sang himself. It was the more gratifying to find the man friendly and wishful to assist. He made it a small affair to find some good plain family of merchants, where Catriona might harbour till the *Rose* was loaden; declared he would then blithely carry her back to Leith for nothing and see her safe in the hands of Mr. Gregory; and in the meanwhile carried us to a late ordinary for the meal we stood in need of. He seemed extremely friendly, as I say, but what surprised me a good deal, rather boisterous in the bargain; and the cause of this was soon to appear.

===Chinese===

「咔嗒马车」——一种装着长条座位的长车——载着我们走了四个钟头，到了大城市鹿特丹。天早黑透了，街上灯却点得挺亮，挤满野模野样、异国他乡的人物——大胡子的希伯来人、黑人，还有成群的妓女，涂脂抹粉打扮得极不体面，拽着水手的袖子就把人拉住；四下里谈话声嗡嗡地撞，撞得我们脑袋发晕；而最想不到的是：我们看这些外国人稀奇，他们看我们竟也一般稀奇。我尽力摆出好看的脸色，为了姑娘，也为了我自家的体面；可实情是，我觉得自己像只迷途的羊，心在胸膛里焦得怦怦跳。有一两回我打听港口，打听「玫瑰号」的泊位；可要么撞上的只说荷兰话，要么我自己的法语不争气。瞎撞进一条街，正碰上一条挂灯的房子巷子，门口窗口挤满了胭粉气刺鼻的涂面女人；我们走过时她们推推掳掳、说说笑笑，我倒庆幸我们听不懂她们的话。再走一程，我们出到沿岸的一片空场上。
「这下有指望了，」我一望见桅杆便叫道。「就在这港口边走吧。总能碰上几个会英语的；运气好些，说不定正撞上那条船。」
事情的结果仅逊于此：晚上九点前后，我们一头撞进了谁的怀里？桑船长。他告诉我们，这一趟航程快得难以置信，一路风势不减直到进港；因此船上的乘客早已各奔前程去了。要追格比夫妇去上德意志是不可能的，我们再没有别的熟人可依靠，只有桑船长本人。所幸此人热心，又乐于帮忙，这就更叫人宽心了。他说，要找一户正经质村的商人人家安置卡特琳娜、「玫瑰号」装货期间好有个落脚，是桩小事；又拍胸脯说到时乐意分文不取把她带回利斯，亲手交到格雷戈里先生手里；当下便领我们去一家开到深夜的餐馆，吃我们急需的一顿饭。如我所说，他热心得不得了，只是另有一点叫我颇为吃惊：他还相当闹腾；这里头的缘故，很快就见分晓。

===Original===

For at the ordinary, calling for Rhenish wine and drinking of it deep, he soon became unutterably tipsy. In, this case, as too common with all men, but especially with those of his rough trade, what little sense or manners he possessed deserted him; and he behaved himself so scandalous to the young lady, jesting most ill-favoredly at the figure she had made on the ship’s rail, that I had no resource but carry her suddenly away.
She came out of that ordinary clinging to me close. “Take me away, David,” she said. “*You* keep me. I am not afraid with you.”
“And have no cause, my little friend!” cried I, and could have found it in my heart to weep.
“Where will you be taking me?” she said again. “Don’t leave me at all events, never leave me.”
“Where am I taking you indeed?” says I stopping, for I had been staving on ahead in mere blindness. “I must stop and think. But I’ll not leave you, Catriona; the Lord do so to me, and more also, if I should fail or fash you.”
She crept closer in to me by way of a reply.
“Here,” I said, “is the stillest place that we have hit on yet in this busy byke of a city. Let us sit down here under yon tree and consider of our course.”
That tree (which I am little like to forget) stood hard by the harbour side. It was a black night, but lights were in the houses, and nearer hand in the quiet ships; there was a shining of the city on the one hand, and a buzz hung over it of many thousands walking and talking; on the other, it was dark and the water bubbled on the sides. I spread my cloak upon a builder’s stone, and made her sit there; she would have kept her hold upon me, for she still shook with the late affronts; but I wanted to think clear, disengaged myself, and paced to and fro before her, in the manner of what we call a smuggler’s walk, belabouring my brains for any remedy. By the course of these scattering thoughts I was brought suddenly face to face with a remembrance that, in the heat and haste of our departure, I had left Captain Sang to pay the ordinary. At this I began to laugh out loud, for I thought the man well served; and at the same time, by an instinctive movement, carried my hand to the pocket where my money was. I suppose it was in the lane where the women jostled us; but there is only the one thing certain, that my purse was gone.
“You will have thought of something good,” said she, observing me to pause.

===Chinese===

在餐馆里，他要了莱茵酒，大口大口灌下去，不多时就醉得没个样了。人一醉就这样——这也是男人们的通病，他那等粗行当里尤其如此——他本来就不多的见识和体统全数弃他而去；他对着年轻小姐的言行不堪入耳，拿她当初在船舷上的模样极难听地打趣，我别无他法，只好猛地把她带走。
出得餐馆，她紧紧贴着我。「带我走，戴维，」她说。「有*你*护着我。同你在一起，我不怕。」
「也不必有半点怕，我的小朋友！」我叫道，心里几乎要哭出来。
「你要带我去哪儿？」她又说。「千万别丢下我，永远别丢下我。」
「我这是要带你去哪儿呢？」我停下步来说——方才我纯是瞎走。「得停下想一想。可我不会丢下你，卡特琳娜；我若负你、烦扰你，愿上帝这样待我、加倍地待我。」
她往我身边又保近了些，权作回答。
「这儿，」我说，「是我们在这座闹哄哄的蜂窠城里撞见的最清净的地方。就在那棵树下坐坐，商量商量咱们的路数。」
那棵树（我是万万忘不了的）就立在港口边上。夜很黑，但屋子里有灯，更近处安静的船上也有；一边是满城的灯火，罩着成千上万人走动谈话的嗡嗡声；另一边是黑，水在船舷两侧汩汩地冒泡。我把斗篷铺在一块建筑用石上，让她坐下；她还想抓着我不放，方才的屈辱还叫她发着抖；可我要想个清楚，便抽出身来，在她面前来回踱步，踱的是我们所说的「走私客的步子」，绞尽脑汁找一条出路。顺着这些七零八落的念头，我猛然迎面撞上一桩回想：方才仓皇离开，饭钱竟留给了桑船长。想到这里我放声大笑，只觉这汉子活该；同时，凭一个本能的动作，我伸手去摸装钱的口袋。多半是在那条巷子里，被女人们推搡时丢的；但有一件事确凿无疑：我的钱袋没了。
「你准是想出好法子了吧。」她见我停住，说。

===Original===

At the pinch we were in, my mind became suddenly clear as a perspective glass, and I saw there was no choice of methods. I had not one doit of coin, but in my pocketbook I had still my letter on the Leyden merchant; and there was now but the one way to get to Leyden, and that was to walk on our two feet.
“Catriona,” said I, “I know you’re brave and I believe you’re strong, do you think you could walk thirty miles on a plain road?” We found it, I believe, scarce the two-thirds of that, but such was my notion of the distance.
“David,” she said, “if you will just keep near, I will go anywhere and do anything. The courage of my heart, it is all broken. Do not be leaving me in this horrible country by myself, and I will do all else.”
“Can you start now and march all night?” said I.
“I will do all that you can ask of me,” she said, “and never ask you why. I have been a bad ungrateful girl to you; and do what you please with me now! And I think Miss Barbara Grant is the best lady in the world,” she added, “and I do not see what she would deny you for at all events.”
This was Greek and Hebrew to me; but I had other matters to consider, and the first of these was to get clear of that city on the Leyden road. It proved a cruel problem; and it may have been one or two at night ere we had solved it. Once beyond the houses, there was neither moon or stars to guide us; only the whiteness of the way in the midst and a blackness of an alley on both hands. The walking was besides made most extraordinary difficult by a plain black frost that fell suddenly in the small hours and turned that highway into one long slide.
“Well, Catriona,” said I, “here we are like the king’s sons and the old wives’ daughters in your daft-like Highland tales. Soon we’ll be going over the ‘seven Bens, the seven glens, and the seven mountain moors.’ ” Which was a common byword or overcome in these tales of hers that had stuck in my memory.
“Ah,” says she, “but here are no glens or mountains! Though I will never be denying but what the trees and some of the plain places hereabouts are very pretty. But our country is the best yet.”
“I wish we could say as much for our own folk,” says I, recalling Sprott and Sang, and perhaps James More himself.
“I will never complain of the country of my friend,” said she, and spoke it out with an accent so particular that I seemed to see the look upon her face.

===Chinese===

处在我们那样的窘境，我的脑子忽然像望远镜一样透亮：办法没有第二个可挑。我身上一枚小铜板也没有，可记事本里还有那封写给莱顿商人的信；眼下到莱顿只剩一条路——靠我们自己的两条腿。
「卡特琳娜，」我说，「我知道你勇敢，也信你有力气——平路上走三十英里，你觉得行么？」后来我们量下来，恐怕不足那段路的三分之二；可我当时对路程的估量便是如此。
「戴维，」她说，「只要你别走远，去哪儿、干什么我都依。我心里的胆气，全碎掉了。别把我一个人掠在这个可怕的国里，其余什么都行。」
「现在就动身，走一整夜，成么？」我说。
「你求我做什么我都做，」她说，「而且绝不问你为什么。我从前待你是个又坏又不知好歹的姑娘；如今随你怎样发落我！我想芭芭拉·格兰特小姐是天下最好的小姐，」她补一句，「我看她无论如何没有什么会拒绝你的。」
这话于我如希腊文希伯来文；可我有别的事要掂量，头一件就是顺着去莱顿的路脱出这座城。这竟是道残酷的难题；等我们解开它，怕已是深夜一两点钟。出了房屋，既无月亮也无星星引路；只有正中一道白白的路，两边是黑洞洞的树巷。加上后半夜骤然降了一场干冷的黑霜，把那条大路冻成了一条长长的滑道，走路难上加难。
「嗐，卡特琳娜，」我说，「咱们这会儿，倒像你那些疯疯癫癫的高地故事里的王子同老太婆的女儿了。眼看就要翻过『七座山、七道谷、七片野甸子』了。」——她那些故事里常有这么句套话（或收梢），一直粘在我记忆里。
「嗐，」她说，「可这儿没有谷，也没有山！虽说这一带的树木、有些平地，确也好看，这个我不否认。可咱们国家还是天下最好的。」
「咱们自己人若也配得上这句话就好了。」我说，想起斯普罗特和桑，或许还有詹姆斯·莫尔本人。
「我朋友的国，我永远不说半个不字。」她说，那语气殊为特别，我仿佛看见了她说这话时脸上的神情。

===Original===

I caught in my breath sharp and came near falling (for my pains) on the black ice.
“I do not know what *you* think, Catriona,” said I, when I was a little recovered, “but this has been the best day yet! I think shame to say it, when you have met in with such misfortunes and disfavours; but for me, it has been the best day yet.”
“It was a good day when you showed me so much love,” said she.
“And yet I think shame to be happy too,” I went on, “and you here on the road in the black night.”
“Where in the great world would I be else?” she cried. “I am thinking I am safest where I am with you.”
“I am quite forgiven, then?” I asked.
“Will you not forgive me that time so much as not to take it in your mouth again?” she cried. “There’s is nothing in this heart to you but thanks. But I will be honest too,” she added, with a kind of suddenness, “and I’ll never can forgive that girl.”
“Is this Miss Grant again?” said I. “You said yourself she was the best lady in the world.”
“So she will be, indeed!” says Catriona. “But I will never forgive her for all that. I will never, never forgive her, and let me hear tell of her no more.”
“Well,” said I, “this beats all that ever came to my knowledge; and I wonder that you can indulge yourself in such bairnly whims. Here is a young lady that was the best friend in the world to the both of us, that learned us how to dress ourselves, and in a great manner how to behave, as anyone can see that knew us both before and after.”
But Catriona stopped square in the midst of the highway.
